Button stays `position: absolute` in all states — in ai-active: `left: var(--space-4); right: var(--space-4)` (spans full bar), `white-space: nowrap; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; text-align: left`. Bar remains exactly 34px. AI prompt shortened from 20→18 words. |
| **2.45.7** | **Fix: triage buttons now render in DM Mono.** `.triage-btn` had no explicit `font-family` — browsers don't inherit font-family onto `<button>` elements from the body, so they fell back to system UI. Added `font-family: var(--font-mono)` to `.triage-btn`. Affects all triage actions (Review, Keep, Let go, Done, Soon). |
| **2.45.6** | **Redesign: thinking state and answer now live inside the timer bar.** When ✦ ask is tapped, the timer bar expands (`max-height: 80px`, inner `height: auto`), fill/time/paused elements fade out, and the button element itself changes content: `✦ ask` → `thinking…` (`.loading` class: xs font, muted color) → question text (sm font, var(--text), normal white-space, tap to dismiss). `_focusAskAI` toggle now checks `timerEl.classList.contains('ai-active')`. `_focusResetAI` no longer references `focusAIQ` (removed). `.focus-ai-q` div and all associated CSS removed. Hints line (`kbdHint`) unchanged — stays showing `space breathe / esc rest`. |
| **2.45.5** | **Redesign: ✦ ask button moved into the timer bar, left-aligned, styled like the copy button (transparent border, monospace, xs, no background).** Button now lives in `.focus-timer-inner` as an absolute-positioned element at `left: var(--space-4)`, vertically centered — opposite side from the countdown. CSS sibling selector `#focusPaused.show ~ .focus-ai-timer-btn` hides it when paused (they share the same left position); `.focus-timer.complete` hides it when session is done; `.focus-timer.ai-active` dims it while fetching. `focusAIBtn` changed from `const` to `let` (assigned after timerEl is created). `_focusResetAI` now also removes `ai-active` from `timerEl`. `_focusAskAI` adds `ai-active` to both `kbdHint` and `timerEl`. Timeout increased from 15s to 25s (cold-start Netlify + AI inference was occasionally hitting the limit); `clearTimeout` added on success path. Question answer still appears in `.focus-ai-q` below the timer bar (inside kbdHint). |
| **2.45.4** | **Fix: Trello task checkbox/title misalignment on desktop.** `.task-meta` (the checklist/due badge container) is `display: inline-block` with `margin-top: var(--space-1)` (4px). In an inline formatting context, `margin-top` on a baseline-aligned inline-block extends the margin box above the text baseline — the line box grows upward, but text characters remain at their normal position. This made the first line of `.task-text` 4px taller at the top, so the text visually appeared ~4px lower within the div while the checkbox (flex `align-items: flex-start`) stayed at the absolute row top. Manual tasks have no `.task-meta`, so no misalignment. Fix: removed `margin-top: var(--space-1)` from `.task-meta`. The margin was added in v2.42.2 for breathing room when the badge wraps on mobile — but since mobile wrapping puts the badge on a separate line (not line 1 where the checkbox aligns), the margin-top only matters on line 2+ and was safe to remove from the global rule. |
| **2.45.3** | **Fix: focus ✦ ask button inline with hints (bordered pill), AI fetch actually works now.** Two fixes: (1) Visual — reverted `display: block` to inline, added `border: 1px solid var(--border); border-radius: var(--radius-sm); padding: 1px var(--space-2)` to make it read as an interactive button without breaking the hint line. Hover also brightens border. (2) AI fetch — the critical bug: focus call was sending `key` as the JSON property name, but the Netlify function destructures `apiKey`. Working calls (nudge etc.) all correctly send `apiKey: key`. This meant the user's localStorage key was never reaching the server — the server used its env var if set, otherwise returned "No API key" error → `_parseAIText` → null → `_focusResetAI()` silently. Also added 15s `AbortController` timeout (no-timeout means a stalled request shows "thinking…" forever) and `res.ok` guard consistent with other AI call sites. |
| **2.45.2** | **Fix: focus ✦ ask button redesigned — legible, labeled, on its own line.** Three problems in one: (1) opacity stacking — `.focus-kbd-hint.show` at 0.40 × child opacities caused "thinking…" at 0.11 effective opacity and answer text at 0.32. Fixed by setting container to `opacity: 1` and moving opacity onto each child directly: kbd hints get `--opacity-label` (0.40), button gets `--opacity-muted` (0.65), question text gets `1`. (2) Button label — bare `✦` didn't read as interactive. Changed to `✦ ask` on its own `display: block` line below the kbd hints, with `width: 100%` for a full-width click target. (3) `system:` field name — `_focusAskAI` was sending `system:` but the Netlify function reads `systemPrompt:`, so the AI ran without its system instructions. Fixed. `ai-active` dim values also tightened: 0.2 → 0.08 now that the container is at 1 instead of 0.40. |
| **2.45.1** | **Fix: ✦ focus question button was nearly invisible due to double opacity stacking.** The `.focus-kbd-hint` container renders at `--opacity-label` (0.40) when shown. The `.focus-ai-btn` inside it also had `opacity: var(--opacity-label)` — multiplicative CSS stacking produced ~16% effective opacity (0.40 × 0.40), versus the `<kbd>` hint items which carried no own opacity and rendered at 0.40. The button was present in the DOM but invisible without knowing to look. Fix: `opacity: 1` on `.focus-ai-btn` (renders at the same 0.40 as the kbd items) and `color: var(--text)` on hover instead of opacity brightening (which was 0.40 × 0.80 = 0.32 — dimmer than the default kbd items, a hover that made things worse). |
| **2.45.0** | **Feature: tap ✦ during a focus session to get a quiet question from your companion.** While focused on a task, a `✦` button sits at the end of the `space breathe / esc rest` hint line. Tapping it dims the hints and fetches a single question — the kind a thoughtful friend asks before you disappear into work. The question is generated from the task text, reads its real meaning in shorthand, and aims at a challenge behind it or what "done enough" looks like for this session. Tap the question to dismiss and restore the hints. Silent fail if AI not configured. Reset on every `closeUI`. |
| **2.44.1** | **Fix: focused task jumped to top of viewport after focus ended (BUG-067).** Can: "when i came back when focus was over, the task moved to the top of the view, and almost out of viewport on the scroll list." **Root cause:** focus mode locks scroll via `position:fixed; top:-${scrollY}px` on body, saving `scrollY` at lock time. While focus is active, `visibilitychange` on tab return triggers `syncDropbox()` → `mergeRemoteData()` → `renderManual()`, which can reorder the task list (the focused task just got `lastActive = Date.now()`, potentially moving it up). On close, `closeUI` restores `scrollY` correctly — but the task is now at a different document offset, so at the same scroll position it appears near the top edge instead of where the user left it. **Fix:** `openUI` now saves the task's viewport Y (`getBoundingClientRect().top`) into `dataset.focusTaskTop` at lock time, after any nudge scroll. `closeUI` reads this back after scroll restoration, compares to the task's actual current viewport Y, and if drift exceeds 2px applies a second `window.scrollTo` to compensate exactly. No change when the DOM didn't move (delta ≈ 0). Deliberately does not center — centering on every close would be scroll hijack; this restores the task to the exact visual row it occupied when focus started. |
| **2.44.0** | **Feature: meeting recorder pops out into a floating window, so capture can be started mid screen-share.** Can's case, verbatim: "i was sharing screen and didnt have privacy or time to start the mic icon on today without showing my task list or stop sharing my screen." Starting capture was not inconvenient — it was *impossible* without either exposing the task list on a shared screen or dropping the share. **Why PiP specifically:** while Meet owns focus, TODAY's page does not, so keyboard shortcuts are out (they need page focus — this also answers the `Backlog` "keyboard shortcuts, revisit if a real workflow gap shows up" row: this is the gap, and shortcuts still do not solve it). A Document PiP window stays always-on-top and interactive while another app has focus. **Privacy comes from content, not concealment:** the window renders only a dot, elapsed time and one button, so it is safe to be *seen* — which is what matters, since a fully shared screen hides nothing anyway. **Deliberately dropped:** opening the meeting link from the PiP ("join & record" in one click). It was the single fragile dependency (`window.open()` from a PiP document) and Can had already offered the alternative — click record, open the link yourself. Calendar-sourced links deferred with it; Can noted the link "could be missing always", which is true of Zoom/Teams events and of events with no conferencing at all. **No manual opener — built, then removed the same day on Can's call:** "i think mvp we should not place a button on the task entry area as pop out button.. automatic is the way, otherwise there is mic icon that does better." Exactly right: to press a pop-out button you must already be looking at TODAY, and from there the mic icon does the same job in fewer steps — the button solved nothing. The PiP only earns its place if it arrives without you going to get it, so it now opens **automatically on tab-leave while a capture is live**. **Best-effort by design:** `requestWindow()` needs transient user activation, so the open rides the activation from the click that started the capture — tap record, switch to the call, it lands; linger in TODAY first and it may not. Immediate attempt plus a 300ms retry, the same caveat and the same shape the focus-timer PiP already carries (documented at its own call site). **Implementation:** `_meetingPipOpen/_meetingPipSync/_meetingPipClose` behind `_meetingInit()`'s existing gate (meeting supported + Gemini key) plus Document PiP support, so Chrome/Edge desktop only. State syncs off the existing wall-clock meeting tick, already immune to background throttling. Closed at `_meetingStop()` rather than `_meetingTeardown()` — teardown only runs once the review is accepted or discarded, so hooking it there would have left the pill floating through the entire review. First-use name prompt lives in the opener and would be invisible from the PiP, so the PiP surfaces the reason instead of appearing to do nothing. **Verified before building:** `MediaRecorder` in a backgrounded tab produced 12 chunks in 6s at a 500ms timeslice, max gap 543ms — no throttling, which was the assumption that could have killed the whole design. Then driven end to end: PiP opens 240×78 idle, record inside the PiP takes `_mtg.live` true with the label and dot following, stop returns it to idle with the window still open. |
| **2.43.9** | **Fix: Trello card rows on mobile — reclaimed 32px of dead width, and stopped the badge pair splitting across lines.** Can: "can it be one row as much as possible on mobile with pomodoro icon and checklist count." Measured and screenshotted at 375px before changing anything, which showed the real mechanic was not what the numbers suggested. **Find 1 — dead space.** `.task-text` carries `padding-right: var(--space-8)` (32px) solely to keep text clear of `.task-copy`, which is `position: absolute` and defined entirely inside `@media (hover: hover)`. On touch that button can never render, so the padding reserved room for nothing — roughly 10 characters of a 375px row. Reclaimed under `@media (hover: none)`, the exact complement, so desktop focus-mode keeps its clearance (verified: desktop `padding-right: 32px` with `.task-copy: block`, touch `0px` with `.task-copy: none`). **Find 2 — the badges were separable.** The screenshot showed the actual ugliness was not the wrap itself: a line break could occur *between* `.task-meta` and `.session-count`, leaving the checklist on line 1 and orphaning `2 🍅` alone on line 2. Reclaiming the padding alone made this *more* likely, not less, by moving the break point. Fixed by wrapping the pair in a new `.task-tail` span with `white-space: nowrap`, so it fits on the title's line or moves to the next line whole. Mirrored in `renderTrello()`'s patch path per Rule 27 (both already built the same order after v2.42.2); design-lint's parity check passes. **Where this ends:** a title long enough to fill the row still pushes the pair to line 2 — together now rather than split, which is the correct behaviour. Guaranteeing one row for any title would need title truncation; proposed and **rejected same day** — the task text is the content you act on, and hiding its tail to win a row trades legibility and affordance for tidiness. Recorded in `Backlog.md` → Not implementing so it is not re-proposed. |
| **2.43.8** | **Fix: focus minutes from another device read 0 on the second device, and polluted yesterday's history (BUG-066).** Can's report: worked a session on desktop PWA, checked mobile PWA later the same day, focus minutes showed 0. **Root cause:** `mergeRemoteData()` adopted the remote value but never wrote `stat_focus_mins_date` alongside it. `applyNewDayCleanup()` runs *after* the Dropbox restore by explicit design (`init()`: "so we always clean the freshest data") — it read the stale date, concluded the freshly-merged minutes must be yesterday's, banked them into `today_daily_history` as yesterday's entry, and zeroed the counter. Two harms from one omission: today's minutes vanish on the second device's first open of the day, and yesterday's history is overwritten with today's number. **Not a regression** — the merge has never stamped that date. Before BUG-063 (v2.42.4) the cleanup reset unconditionally, so it wiped harder still; that fix added the right shape of guard but could not help when nothing wrote the date it reads. **Fix:** the merge now stamps `stat_focus_mins_date = _getAppDay()` whenever it adopts a value. Safe because `mergedFocusMins` only differs from local when remote's date-gated today-value won, so the merged number is definitionally today's. Plus one guard for the case the stamp would otherwise cost: if the local counter was still on yesterday with unbanked minutes, those are handed to `stat_focus_mins_yesterday_snapshot` — the same channel BUG-063 established — so history keeps them. **Verified** by driving `mergeRemoteData()` with a synthetic payload in headless Chrome (no Dropbox, no network) across four cases: desktop-50/mobile-idle → 50 survives cleanup; mobile holding 40 unbanked → today 50 *and* yesterday keeps 40; local 60 vs remote 50 → max still wins; remote holding yesterday's 45 → never restores as today (BUG-024 guard intact). All four are now permanent scenarios in `scripts/focus-test.mjs`, whose sync section is validated by reverting the date stamp and confirming exit 1. |
| **2.43.7** | **Fix: focus mode re-opened itself after you left it; timer bar torn loose on fast task switch (BUG-065 — regression from v2.43.0).** Can's report: switching focus between tasks quickly breaks things, focus returns to a task after leaving it, and "focus component leftovers stay on the screen." He suspected sync — close: not Dropbox or the Trello focus map (v2.43.6 not implicated), but the 7-second sync tick, because that is what drives `renderTrello()`/`renderManual()`. **Root cause (reproduced in headless Chrome before any edit):** `closeUI(doResetState)` cleared `today_focus_session` only when `doResetState` was true — but every user-facing exit passes **false**: Escape (12745), click-outside (12676), task switch (12244, inside `openUI`), PiP close (13029). Only `_focusOnCheck` passed true. So unless you *completed* a task, a live-looking session stayed in localStorage; `_tryRestoreFocusSession()` — wired into `renderManual()`/`renderTrello()` by v2.43.0 — then found it, saw `uiTaskId` was null so its only guard passed, and silently re-opened focus on the task you had just left. Demonstrated with no switching at all: focus a task, press Escape, call `renderManual()` → focus is open again. Self-perpetuating, since the restored session re-saves on every tick. **Second, older fault:** `closeUI` defers DOM teardown 200ms for the CSS transition, while `openUI` calls `closeUI(false)` synchronously on switch — so the stale teardown fired *after* the new session was set up and ran `document.body.appendChild(timerEl)`, reparenting the timer out from under the live task, plus `appEl.classList.remove('focusing')`. Latent since long before v2.43.0 and previously self-healing via `_focusReanchor()`; v2.43.0 made the same window destructive. **Fixes:** (1) `today_focus_session` is now cleared synchronously on *every* close — the key means "a session is in flight", and any close ends it. (2) `_tryRestoreFocusSession()` is cold-start-only via a `_restoreAttempted` latch: one decision per page load, deliberately *not* set when the task element is missing so the Trello-not-loaded-yet retry still works. (3) A `focusGen` counter, bumped by every `openUI`/`closeUI`, lets the deferred teardown detect that a newer session superseded it and skip the shared timer chrome — while still releasing its own task, and never stripping `.focused` off whatever is live now (the A→B→A-inside-200ms case). Also re-asserts `_saveSession` when re-opening a paused task, since the close inside `openUI` now clears the key. **Verified** in headless Chrome across five scenarios: Escape-then-render no longer re-opens; A→B switch keeps the timer anchored under B through the teardown window (was `<body>`, focusing=false); rapid A→B→A settles on A; a render tick during a live session leaves it untouched; Escape leaves no stale key. |
| **2.43.6** | **Fix: focusing a Trello card masked its age for one day, then it came back dimmed *worse* than before (BUG-064).** Can's report: an aged Trello card un-ages after a focus session, but ages again the day after, "not sure to which level." Investigated before changing anything — the level is whatever the untouched first-seen date implies, which is always at least the level it was at before, because the clock never stopped. **Root cause:** the two task types un-aged by different mechanisms. A manual task's age basis is `task.lastActive || created`, and focus sets `lastActive = Date.now()` — the basis genuinely *moves*. A Trello card's basis is `_getTrelloFirstSeen()[id]`, which never moves; instead `taskHTML()` carried a display override, `if (focusCount > 0) ageDays = 0`, fed by `today_trello_focus` — a map that is **wiped every midnight**. So focus was a 24-hour cosmetic mask with the clock still running underneath. Measured: a card first seen 6 days ago reads `mid`, is focused, reads clean that day, then next morning reads `old` — one tier *worse* than before the work. This was never in BUG-043's scope; that fix was about a card staying dimmed *while* you work on it, and the code comment at the override openly notes "manual tasks un-age on activity but Trello can't." **Fix:** new `today_trello_lastactive` map (`{trello_<id>: ms}`), pushed forward at both focus sites (`closeUI` partial session and `_logSession` completed pomodoro), making the basis `lastActive || firstSeen || now` — structurally identical to the manual path. **Why a separate key rather than writing into `today_trello_firstseen`:** first-seen MIN-merges across devices (earliest sighting wins — that is its meaning), so activity written into it would be reverted by any device still holding the older timestamp. The new map MAX-merges instead, mirroring how manual tasks already reconcile `lastActive` in `mergeRemoteData()`. Added to the backup payload, the merge path, and full-restore; pruned alongside first-seen in `loadTrello()` so it stays bounded; explicitly excluded from the midnight clear. The BUG-043 override is left in place — harmless now (it only ever fires when `lastactive` is also set) and it preserves that fix's contract for partial sessions. **Verified** by extracting the real `_trelloAgeBasis`/`_markTrelloActive` out of `index.html` and exercising them: day 7 after focus now reads `1d`/no bucket instead of `old`; untouched cards still age normally from first-seen; an older remote value no longer reverts the basis. No migration needed — an empty `lastactive` falls through to first-seen, so existing cards show their true age until first focused. |
| **2.43.5** | **Fix: two shipped syntax errors, plus morning-nudge prompt rebalanced.** **The syntax errors are the headline.** v2.43.1 wrote `"N didn't happen"` with a raw apostrophe inside a single-quoted JS string; v2.43.4 wrote a literal newline inside `'Habits:
'` in `insights.js` (a Python heredoc ate the escape). Either one kills the entire script — the app would not boot. Both reached `master`. The edits were not the failure; the process was: five versions shipped without running `scripts/smoke-test.mjs`, which boots a real page and asserts no uncaught errors, and catches both in seconds. Also trimmed the in-app `CHANGELOG` from 8 entries back to 3 (Rule 31), and mirrored v2.43.3/v2.43.4 here, both of which had been skipped across the same run. **Prompt rebalance:** reconstructed the actual facts block `_fetchDayNudgeAI()` sends and measured it — `_memoryForAI()`'s "About this person" was 56% of the prompt and byte-identical every day, against 35% for the actual list. A surface whose Wallpaper escape is "different each time" had majority-constant input. Fixes: (1) `_memoryForAI(scope)` — `'nudge'` drops lifetime trophies (best streak, total focus hours, moments, days active) that cannot change what is said about *today*; the conversational assistant still calls it bare and keeps them, where they read as legitimate color. (2) entries join with `
` instead of `' '` — four entries are themselves multi-line, so a space join welded each one's tail onto the next entry's heading (`...focusing on it (2026-07-22) Recent conversations:`). Ratio moved 56%→52%, deliberately modest: the remaining entries all earn their place. The real wins are dead weight removed and section boundaries restored. **Not verified against live model output** — needs a device with an API key; tracked in Backlog → Watching. |
| **2.43.4** | **Feature: `_memoryForAI()` gains four behavioral signals.** Can asked for late-additions, task lifespan, habit context, and a 7-day history summary. (1) **Habits** — active habits with per-habit 7-day completion count and a done-yesterday flag; the AI previously had zero habit visibility. (2) **7-day rhythm** — average tasks/day and focus/day from `today_daily_history`, plus a trend clause against the prior 7 days. (3) **Task lifespan** — `_memoryOnTaskComplete()` now also takes `taskId`, computes days from `_getCreatedFromId()` to completion, and keeps a rolling 20-sample average, so "4 days old" can be read against this person's own norm. (4) **Late-additions** — surfaced from the already-tracked `lateAdditions` array when ≥40% of tasks are added after 2pm. Current streak added alongside best streak. Two call sites updated to pass `taskId`. |
| **2.43.3** | **Feature: morning-nudge prompt rewritten purpose-first; writing style inferred from completions.** The instruction had grown into a ~200-word ranked decision tree ("focus sessions are the strongest signal", "only mention a task when…") — rules that constrained the model's judgment rather than sharpening it. Replaced with ~80 words of purpose: *"Find the one thing worth saying that they'd miss just by reading the list themselves"* + *"understand what each task means in real life — what depends on it, what happens if they wait, who else might be involved, whether the window is closing."* No signal hierarchy. Prompted by Can's push that the AI should grasp the task *sentence*, not scan for keywords: "book hotel" is time-bound by nature with no date language in it. Separately, `_memoryForAI()` now sends the last 5 completed task texts verbatim instead of keyword frequencies — "call dentist", "fix nav bug in prod" tells the model far more about this person's shorthand than `themes: call, fix`. System prompt gained one line: *"Task text is written in the user's own shorthand — read the full meaning from context."* |
| **2.43.2** | **Feature: morning nudge gains Soon list visibility and text-based urgency reading.** Previously `_fetchDayNudgeAI()` only sent today's manual tasks and Trello cards — `soonTasks` was never in the prompt, so the AI had no way to surface a deferred item even if it had been sitting with time-sensitive language for weeks. Now: (1) `soonLines` builds a block of up to 6 Soon tasks with age-in-soon (from `zoneChangedAt`), total task age (from id-encoded creation), focus sessions spent before deferral, and whether the task returned from Past — rich context beyond just sitting length. (2) The instruction now tells the AI to read the actual task text for urgency and time-sensitivity across *both* lists — date references, event names, trip mentions, "before X" phrases are signals even without formal due dates. (3) A Soon task is only worth surfacing if the text or signals give a clear reason (time-sensitive language, long deferral with prior focus effort, returned from past) — it's a deferred intent, not an abandoned one. Same applies to today's tasks: the AI is now told to read the text for context, not just count sessions and age. |
| **2.43.1** | **Fix: triage counter now reads "N didn't happen" throughout.** During triage, the title opened as "N didn't happen" (set by `triageExpand()`), then on the first decision switched to "N left" (set by `renderTriageList()`) — two different framings, with "left" being vague (left to do what?). Changed `renderTriageList()` to keep using "N didn't happen" so the counter counts down in the same language it opens with, and "1 didn't happen" on the last task reads clearly as "one task still needs a decision." |
| **2.43.0** | **Feature: focus sessions now survive iOS killing the app in the background.** The session state (`taskStates`) was always in-memory only — a comment in the code even said "NOT persisted to localStorage — resets on page reload intentionally." When iOS reclaims memory from a backgrounded PWA and Can returns, the app reloads fresh: task list appears, no focus mode, no time recorded. Fix: `_saveSession()` writes `{ taskId, rem, savedAt, paused }` to `today_focus_session` localStorage on every tick (every second), on `start()`, and on `pause()`. On reload, `_tryRestoreFocusSession()` (called from both `renderManual()` and `renderTrello()` so it handles both task types) checks the persisted state: if the task no longer exists or is done, clears; if the session would have already completed while away (`adjustedRem ≤ 0`), calls `_recordFocusComplete()` silently — no chime, no UI, just stats updated and 🍅 logged; if time still remains, re-opens the timer UI with the correct remaining time and resumes ticking. Also factored the recording logic from `completeFor()` into shared `_recordFocusComplete()` to avoid repeating the BUG-063 day-boundary guard. |
| **2.42.4** | **Fix: focus sessions completing just after midnight were wiped by a race with the daily reset (BUG-063).** The issue: `completeFor()` or `_trackFocusTime()` would add 25 mins to `stat_focus_mins_today`, then within the same 7-second ticker window `applyNewDayCleanup()` would fire, snapshot that total as "yesterday's" focus, and reset the counter to 0 — leaving the user with no focus recorded for the session. The fix is three-pronged: (1) `completeFor()` and `_trackFocusTime()` now detect a day boundary by comparing `stat_focus_mins_date` to `_getAppDay()` before incrementing — if the date has flipped, they snapshot the pre-midnight total into `stat_focus_mins_yesterday_snapshot` and start fresh from 0 for today; (2) `applyNewDayCleanup()` uses that snapshot for yesterday's daily history entry when `stat_focus_mins_date` is already today, and skips resetting the counter (preserving post-midnight sessions); (3) the Trello focus map (`today_trello_focus`) is similarly guarded — only cleared if `today_trello_focus_date` is not already today's date, so 🍅 session badges from post-midnight sessions also survive the cleanup. |
| **2.42.3** | **Fix: task-list Day Nudge was stuck showing the generic "N tasks carried over" fallback every day, while the real AI insight generated fine and sat unused in the same cache About's Today block reads.** Can's report, precisely diagnosed rather than guessed at: "i think ih have been consistently seeing x amount of tasks carried over... message outcome has been consistent" — asked him to check `localStorage.getItem('day_nudge_ai_<date>')` directly, which came back with a genuinely good AI sentence ("Two focus sessions on the youtube videos and content task, and it's still open — that one's got some resistance to it"), while the task-list nudge had shown him only the plain count. Both surfaces read the exact same cache key; About shows it plainly with no race, so the fact that it worked there proved the AI generation itself was fine. Root cause: `_raceAINudge`'s 1000ms window races a real Netlify+LLM round trip, which routinely takes longer — the fallback wins by default most days, and `_nudgeRendered` then permanently blocks the rest of that page load from checking again, even after the real line finishes generating moments later and gets cached correctly (proven by About reading it fine). Fix: new `_nudgeIsFallback` flag tracks whether the *currently shown* nudge is the plain fallback or real AI text. The render guard changed from "never render again once shown" to "never render again once the *real AI text* has shown" — a fallback may still be upgraded once, by a later natural call site (wake, a later sync tick), to the AI line if it's arrived in the meantime. Deliberately narrow: an AI answer can still never replace another AI answer, and no swap ever happens mid-race or mid-instant — only a plain fallback, on a genuinely later call, may be upgraded once. This doesn't reopen BUG-034 (the "shows a note, then swaps a second later" bug that made `_nudgeRendered` a blanket lock in the first place) — that bug was about *cross-call-site* races stepping on each other within the same load; this fix only fires on a distinctly later, separate re-check. Verified both directions via headless Chrome (hour forced to morning to bypass the noon cutoff): fallback-shown-then-cache-arrives correctly upgrades on the next call; AI-already-shown-then-cache-changes correctly stays unchanged. |
| **2.42.2** | **Fix: v2.42.1's wrap-spacing fix wasn't enough — invisible focus-session marker was crowding the checklist badge; also reordered per Can's request (sessions last).** Can's follow-up, on real-device testing: "it pushed the checklist next row, but then it is really close to the text above. Also, I think in the card there is enough space to fit both pomodoro session icon+number, then the checklist numbers." Reproduced first: `.session-count` is `opacity: 0` at rest (only shown on hover or when done) but still occupies its full inline width even while invisible — and it sat *between* the arrow link and the checklist badge in the DOM, so its phantom width silently ate into the line before the checklist got a chance to fit, forcing an unnecessary wrap purely from something the user couldn't even see. Reordered `taskHTML()`'s template so `.task-meta` (due/checklist) comes right after the link, with `.session-count` moved to the very end — the invisible span's phantom width can now only push *itself* out of the line, never the checklist. Also added `margin-top: var(--space-1)` to `.task-meta` for real breathing room when it does still wrap on a genuinely long title. Fixing this exposed a matching bug in the *other* render path: `renderTrello()`'s 7-second patch-tick logic in `trello.js` built its own `newText` string in the old order (text+link+session, with due/checklist patched as a *separate*, independently-appended step) — after any patch that touched text/link/session, the checklist would get wiped and re-appended at the very end, landing after the session count and silently drifting out of sync with `taskHTML()`'s new order (a live Rule 27 violation, not just a risk). Consolidated into one comparison string built in the same order both paths now share, removing the separate append-if-missing branch entirely. Verified the whole chain — reproduced the original bug via a real `taskHTML()` render at 375px before touching CSS, verified the reorder+spacing fix the same way, then verified the patch-path fix by simulating a live checklist-count change through `renderTrello()` directly and confirming the DOM order stayed correct before and after. |
| **2.42.1** | **Fix: Trello checklist badge landed flush against the container edge, no spacing, when it wrapped to its own line on mobile.** Can's report: "line breaks after text+arrow. It should come a bit of spacing after arrow not below the task list." Reproduced first rather than guessing — rendered a realistic Trello card (long title + link + checklist) via `taskHTML()` at a 375px viewport in headless Chrome and screenshotted it, confirming the exact symptom: the `2/5 ✓` badge (`.task-meta`, wrapping the due/checklist `.badge` spans) appeared on its own line with zero visible indentation, reading as a disconnected row rather than a continuation of the text above. Root cause: `.task-meta { display: inline; margin-left: var(--space-2); }` — a plain inline element's leading margin is suppressed by the browser at a line-wrap point (a real CSS spec behavior, not a bug in any one browser) — so the intended spacing silently disappeared exactly when the badge needed it most (staying on the same line, the margin renders fine; only wrapping to a fresh line loses it). Fix: `display: inline-block` — an atomic inline box whose margin renders correctly whether it stays on the same line or starts a new one, with no other layout change (still wraps at the same point, same font/spacing otherwise). Verified across three title lengths (short — badge stays inline; medium — badge wraps with correct spacing; long, double-wrapping title — same) via headless screenshots before and after. |
| **2.42.0** | **Feature: two-tap share on touch — first tap reveals, second tap shares.** Can's explicit request: "First tap highlight the text and shows share icon, second tap brings up share modal." Root problem being solved: desktop gets this reveal-then-act sequence for free (hover reveals, then a click acts), but touch has no hover — so touch's only option before this was either "always show the label" (visually noisy, already rejected earlier in the poem-share arc) or "tap once and immediately share" (no equivalent of "just glancing at it," which is what hovering lets desktop users do). New `_onPoemTap()` branches on `window.matchMedia('(hover: hover)').matches` — true (desktop) keeps the exact prior single-tap-shares behavior unchanged; false (touch) toggles a new `.revealed` class on `#poemBlock` on first tap (showing the identical highlighted-text + bordered-label visual state hover already produces on desktop, verified via computed-style checks: text color and label opacity/border match the hover-sharpened state exactly) without calling `_shareDailyPoem()`, then calls it on a second tap while still revealed. A document-level click listener collapses `.revealed` when tapping anywhere outside the poem, so a later fresh tap always starts over at "reveal," never lands on a stale "share" from an old interaction. Deliberately did NOT collapse `.revealed` immediately after firing share — the click-flash/`.copied` confirmation from v2.40.8 needs the label to stay visible to actually show that feedback; collapsing right away would hide it before the user could see it. Keyboard activation (Enter/Space) stays single-step, unchanged — a "press Enter twice" requirement would be a real accessibility regression for a device class that already gets a clear focus indicator without needing a reveal step. `.btn-poem-share`'s base `opacity: 0` moved out of the `@media (hover: hover)` block to apply everywhere (previously touch defaulted to always-visible, outside any media query at all — this changes that default specifically to support the new reveal step) with `.poem-block.revealed` as a plain, unconditional CSS rule two-stage-matching the existing hover states. Verified touch and desktop paths independently via headless Chrome — touch via a `window.matchMedia` stub (Puppeteer's `emulateMediaFeatures` doesn't support `hover`/`pointer`), desktop via real default `hover: hover` — confirming zero cross-contamination between the two code paths. |
| **2.41.1** | **Refactor: Roadmap #3 — `error-monitor.js` extracted (seventh module, ~130 lines).** Can asked for an extraction candidate other than `sync.js` (already flagged as the risky Non-Delegation next-up); traced the file for a genuinely self-contained, non-core, non-sync-adjacent block before proposing one. Moved to `assets/error-monitor.js`: `_errorLog`, `_fmtErrTime`, `_showErrorDot`, `_logSyncError`, the `localStorage.setItem` quota-error wrapper, `window.onerror`/`unhandledrejection` handlers, `_errorPanelOpen`/`_toggleErrorPanel`. Verified before touching anything: all 14 external call sites (13 in `index.html`, 1 in `trello.js`) invoke `_logSyncError` from inside a function body or `catch` block — none at parse/eval time — so load order only needs the new script to be defined before those call sites actually *fire* (well after full page load), not before any other script's own top-level execution. New `<script src="assets/error-monitor.js">` placed right after `insights.js`, preserving (marginally improving, never regressing) `window.onerror`'s installation timing versus the pre-extraction inline position. Static CSS for `#errorIndicator`/`#errorPanel` stayed in `index.html`, matching how `sound.js`/`idle.js`/`celebration.js` were extracted — only JS moves. `sw.js` `CACHE_APP_SHELL` gains the new file. Verified end-to-end post-extraction via headless Chrome: a synthetic `_logSyncError()` call shows the red dot with the correct count, the panel renders the message and source badge correctly on click, and a genuine uncaught `throw` is still caught by the relocated `window.onerror`. First extraction in the Roadmap #3 series with zero Non-Delegation concerns — dev-aid logging only, no sync/merge logic. Next: `sync.js` (~510 lines) remains the one that needs a risk discussion before touching. |
| **2.41.0** | **Removed: ✦'s Daily brief — resolved a W3 verdict early (2026-07-28, two days ahead of its 2026-07-30 due date) as a cut, not an iteration.** Follow-up on the same day's Daily-brief evidence-gathering: Can named the actual mechanism — "[it] doesn't really belong under that CTA... i rarely click on the ✦ CTA at the bottom." `#addAiBtn`'s stated identity is "Ask anything" (task entry / AI assistant); revealing a completely unrelated feature (a curated read: the day's nudge + today's poem) only on an *empty* tap of that same control had no discoverable connection to what the button says it does. Two possible fixes followed from that diagnosis — give the brief its own honest entry point, or accept it's redundant with what About already shows (Today block = nudge, Read Me = poem, both available all day through a predictable path) and cut it. Can chose the cut: "i go for the second one." `_showDailyBrief()` (`index.html`) removed entirely along with its supporting CSS (`.brief-container`/`.brief-nudge`/`.brief-poem`/`.brief-poem-author`); `handleBarSubmit()`'s empty-✦-tap branch now calls plain `openAI()` instead, which runs the AI panel's own pre-existing `_aiLoad()` proactive-suggestions default — the button now does what it says it does, nothing composed specially. Verified via headless Chrome with a real (fake-valued but correctly-keyed) AI credential configured, confirming the AI panel opens normally on empty tap and no `.brief-container` markup remains anywhere. `Backlog.md`'s Roadmap #7 status, the ✦ brief's W3 row, and `design/Components.md`'s Daily Brief section all updated to record the removal and its reasoning rather than left describing a feature that no longer exists. |
| **2.40.8** | **Fix: poem share gave no click feedback at all on real devices; the v2.40.7 share-sheet anchor fix is confirmed ineffective and removed.** Can's report after testing v2.40.7: "i dont see any click or clicked state of it after the fix still... share sheet pop up is behaving exactly like before fix." Root cause of the missing click state: `_shareDailyPoem()`'s `navigator.share` branch calls `navigator.share({text}).catch(() => {})` and returns immediately — no text change, no color change, nothing. Every prior round's headless verification had forced `navigator.share` to `undefined` specifically to exercise the clipboard-fallback path (the only one testable without a real OS share sheet), which meant this exact gap was invisible in testing while being the actual path taken on real devices, where `navigator.share` almost always exists. Fixed: `_shareDailyPoem()` now applies a `.clicked` class (accent color/border, same tokens as `.copied`) immediately, synchronously, before either branch runs, reverting after 400ms — visible feedback regardless of which path executes or whether the poem was hovered first. Verified via a headless stub that resolves `navigator.share()` immediately (rather than removing it): `.clicked` applies within 50ms of the click and `navigator.share` is confirmed called, class removed by 500ms. Separately: v2.40.7's theory that focusing the small label before calling `share()` would bias the native popover's anchor point toward it was directly disproven — Can confirmed the popup position is completely unchanged from before that fix, across every version tried so far (button vs. span, corner vs. centered vs. whole-poem-clickable). That's strong evidence the popover's position is entirely OS/browser-controlled and doesn't respond to page DOM structure, element position, or focus state at all. Removed the ineffective `.focus()` call and the now-purposeless `tabindex="-1"` rather than leave misleading dead code claiming to fix something it doesn't — this specific complaint is being left unresolved rather than patched with another unverified guess. |
| **2.40.7** | **Fix: poem share's label wasn't actually identical to `.task-copy`, and the native share sheet opened far from the click point.** Can's follow-up on v2.40.6: "should have the same design as copy so we use the same token and component... also missing the click state... share modal appears many pixels off from the click area." Diffed `.btn-poem-share` against `.task-copy` property-by-property and found real gaps, not just approximations: no `border` at all (so no bordered-box hover state — the "missing click state" was this, `.task-copy:hover`'s `border-color: var(--border)` box, which only fires on hovering the label *itself*, not just the containing block), `top:0; right:0` instead of `.task-copy`'s `var(--space-2)` inset, and no `line-height`/`white-space`/`pointer-events` parity. Rebuilt `.btn-poem-share` to match every declared property and every state (rest, parent-hover reveal at `var(--opacity-copy)`, own-hover sharpen to full opacity + border + text color, `.copied`) exactly — same tokens, same two-stage reveal, only the trigger (whole poem vs. a single task row) differs. For the share-sheet position: `navigator.share()`'s popover placement is OS/browser-controlled, not something CSS or a position argument can set directly — but some implementations anchor it near `document.activeElement` rather than the literal cursor coordinates, and the whole poem being the click target meant that active element was the large multi-line block, an ambiguous anchor reference. `_shareDailyPoem()` now explicitly focuses the small corner label (`#poemShareBtn`, given `tabindex="-1"` so it's programmatically focusable without joining the poem block's own tab stop) immediately before calling `navigator.share()`/clipboard, biasing whatever anchor heuristic the browser uses toward a small, predictable point instead of the poem's full bounding box. Verified via headless Chrome that `document.activeElement` correctly becomes the label after clicking anywhere on the poem text — the actual on-page position of the native OS share sheet itself isn't something headless automation can verify, so this needs Can's real-device confirmation. |
| **2.40.6** | **Feature: poem share, redone to Can's exact spec after v2.40.5's centered-link version still didn't land.** Can's four explicit requirements this round, given verbatim rather than as an open design direction: text highlights like a task on hover, share label on the right like `.task-copy`, label invisible unless hovering the poem, and clicking anywhere on the poem (not just the label) opens the share dialog — with an explicit note that this should be a *simplified* `.task-copy`, no bounding box/background-fill highlight, since the poem isn't draggable the way a task is. Implementation: whole poem wrapped in a `.poem-block` (`role="button" tabindex="0"`, click + Enter/Space handlers, both calling `_shareDailyPoem()` — the inner label is a plain `<span>` now, not a nested `<button>`, so a click on it bubbles to the same handler with no double-firing risk). "Highlight" interpreted as the poem text's own color brightening (`var(--muted)` → `var(--text)` on `#dailyPoem` and `.poem-author`) rather than `.task:hover`'s literal `background: var(--surface); border-color: var(--border)` box treatment — matches Can's explicit "we don't need a bounding box" clarification. Label positioned `position: absolute; top: 0; right: 0` within `.poem-block`, exactly mirroring `.task-copy`'s corner — but this time the multi-line-overlap risk that caused problems in earlier rounds is solved by giving `.poem-block` a **permanent** `padding-right: 44px` gutter (present at rest and on hover both, so nothing shifts size when the label reveals — the specific mechanism that caused v2.40.4's stray-gap bug doesn't apply here, since padding isn't tied to the hover state). Verified all four requirements together via headless Chrome before shipping: opacity 0 at rest, label's right/top edges matching `.poem-block`'s exactly (391px/408px both, confirming true `.task-copy`-style corner placement), text color changing from `rgb(107,107,120)` to `rgb(232,232,236)` on hover, and clicking a point on the poem *text* (not the label) correctly triggering the copied-confirmation state — each state screenshotted. |
| **2.40.5** | **Fix: v2.40.4's `.task-copy`-mirroring redesign introduced five concrete regressions — reverted to a simpler, always-visible treatment instead of patching further.** Can's real-device testing after v2.40.4: "i dont like it... we worked too long on this, make this right this time." Specific issues, each traced to root cause rather than guessed at: (1) **disproportionately long accent line** — the `::before` left-accent line's height was a percentage of `.poem-block`'s box, which scales with content; task rows are short and single-line so the same technique produces a short line there, but a multi-line poem produced a visually heavy, out-of-place long line. (2) **hover color not visibly changing** — the button was only revealed at `opacity: var(--opacity-copy)` (0.45) on parent-hover, and a `muted → text` color swap at 45% opacity against a dark background reads as barely different, even though the underlying color values (`#6b6b78` → `#e8e8ec`) have plenty of contrast at full opacity. (3) **empty gap below the author line** — the button was hidden via `opacity: 0`, not `display: none`, so it kept participating in flex layout and reserving its own height + margin even while invisible, leaving a visible gap before the next section's divider. (4) **not centered, sitting in a bottom-right corner** — deliberately adapted from `.task-copy`'s corner position, but never actually matched the panel's own established rhythm (nothing else in `.panel-haiku` is corner-anchored). (5) **native share-sheet anchor appearing in the wrong location** — `navigator.share()`'s OS-level popover position is generally anchored to the triggering element's position in most browser implementations; moving the button out of simple normal-flow positioning into a flex-column/`align-self: flex-end` construction shifted where the browser anchored it, a regression Can confirmed by comparing directly against the pre-v2.40.4 behavior. Fix: reverted to a plain, always-visible, always-in-flow button — `display: block; width: fit-content; margin: var(--space-2) auto 0;` (genuinely centered, verified: button center x === panel center x in a headless check), no accent line, no opacity-based hide/reveal, no flex-column wrapper. Kept the genuinely good pieces from the whole arc: `.task-copy`'s corner-label typography (mono, `text-xs`, lowercase "share"/"copied") and the `.copied` accent-colored confirmation state — both add no complexity and don't participate in any of the five regressions above. Verified rest/hover/copied states and true horizontal centering via headless Chrome with real mouse events, each screenshotted. `Components.md`'s "Poem Share" section rewritten to document the reversal honestly rather than leaving the superseded v2.40.4 rationale standing as if it were still current — the lesson recorded there for future work: an established pattern's interaction mechanics (hover-reveal, corner overlay, opacity-based hiding) can carry hidden assumptions (short single-line content, fixed absolute positioning, native API anchor behavior) that don't survive being ported to a different kind of content — verify each transferred mechanic against the new context specifically, not just the end visual result. |
| **2.40.4** | **Feature (design iteration): poem share fully adopts the `.task-copy` interaction pattern, not just its hover-reveal timing.** Can's follow-up on v2.40.3 — "definitely better... however not there yet. we have the task copy pattern... but the hover also highlights the content itself. can we incorporate this pattern more?" Two things transferred directly: (1) the parent-hover coupling — hovering the *content*, not a vague nearby container, is what should trigger the reveal, paired with a visible highlight on the content itself so the connection is obvious before you even reach the button; (2) `.task-copy`'s corner-label typography (`var(--font-mono)`, `var(--text-xs)`, `letter-spacing: 0.06em`, lowercase "share"/"copied") instead of the `.poem-author`-derived all-caps styling from v2.40.2. One thing deliberately adapted rather than copied verbatim: `.task-copy` is absolutely positioned top-right, safe to overlay because task text is short and single-line; a poem wraps across multiple lines, so an absolute top-right button risked sitting on the first line of text. Positioned bottom-right *in normal flow* instead (new `.poem-block` wrapper, `flex-direction: column`, `align-self: flex-end` on the button) — also a better semantic fit, since it places the option where your eye lands *after* finishing the poem rather than priming "manage this" before you've read it. Added the same subtle left accent line (`::before`, 2px, `var(--border)`, opacity 0→1 on hover) already used for task/habit rows, on `.poem-block:hover`, so hovering the poem gives the same tactile "this is engaged" feedback task rows give before you even reach the corner button. Also added a `.copied` confirmation state mirroring `.task-copy.copied` exactly (accent color/border, `opacity: 1` even after the mouse leaves so the 1.8s confirmation window isn't cut short by the hover-reveal's own `opacity: 0` default). Verified all states — rest, poem-hover (accent line + 0.45 opacity), button-hover (opacity 1 + border), and copied (accent color, persists post-mouse-leave) — via headless Chrome with real `mouse.move` events, each screenshotted. General principle written into `Components.md`: for any secondary action attached to content meant to be *experienced* rather than *managed*, default to hidden-until-engaged with a coupled highlight on the content, not just the action. |
| **2.40.3** | **Fix: poem share still felt out of place even after toning down its color.** Can's follow-up on v2.40.2 — right about the link being clickable now, but "the cta still feels out of place." Coaching-moment reframe: the issue was never really styling, it's that a share affordance is *permanently visible* next to something meant to be read quietly — visibility itself reframes the poem from "something you're reading" to "something you could be sending," independent of how loud the button looks. The app already has a precedent for exactly this situation: `.task-copy` is `display: none` at rest and only reveals on hover, inside `@media (hover: hover)` (desktop only — the comment there notes focus mode, and by extension this pattern, never activates on touch). Mirrored it: `.btn-poem-share` is now `opacity: 0; pointer-events: none` at rest on hover-capable devices, revealing at `opacity: var(--opacity-copy)` (0.45, same token `.task-copy` uses) on `.panel-haiku:hover`, then `opacity: 1` on the button's own hover — same two-stage reveal-then-sharpen `.task-copy` already uses. Touch devices have no hover to signal "considering this," so they keep the always-visible (already quiet) styling from v2.40.2 unchanged — removing the feature there entirely, to match `.task-copy`'s touch behavior exactly, would have been a worse regression than leaving a small always-visible link, especially given `navigator.share()` is primarily a mobile-native pattern. Verified all three opacity states (0 at rest, 0.45 on poem hover, 1 on button hover) via headless Chrome with real mouse-move events (not just `:hover` CSS class toggling) and screenshotted each. |
| **2.40.2** | **Fix: poem share button too visually dominant, shared link not clickable.** Can's testing feedback on v2.40.0's poem share, two issues. (1) The button had been aligned to the app's one existing text-link convention (`.config-hint a` — `var(--highlight-ui)`, hover-underline) per an earlier request, but that convention is for essential navigational links; next to a contemplative poem it read as loud and competing rather than quiet. Restyled to match `.poem-author` instead — the element it sits directly beneath — same `var(--text-micro)`, `var(--muted)`, uppercase, letter-spacing; hover now shifts to `var(--text)` + underline rather than being colored at rest. Reads as part of the poem's own metadata line (author + share) instead of a floating CTA. Screenshotted before/after to confirm. (2) The shared text's closing line read `today-here.netlify.app` with no URI scheme — many surfaces that auto-linkify plain text (Messages, WhatsApp, Notes) require a full `https://` prefix to reliably detect a link; a bare domain often renders as plain, non-clickable text. Added the scheme. |
| **2.40.1** | **Fix: BUG-060's actual scope was wider than "fresh device."** Can reported the phantom-Trello-card bug was still happening after the v2.37.7 fix. Traced `_reconcileTrelloAfterMerge()`'s call sites and found it was only wired into one of what turned out to be the relevant merge paths — the cold-start load. Read `syncAll()`'s 7s ticker end to end to confirm the real mechanism: `syncTrello()` only re-fetches when Trello's own `dateLastActivity` changes, and a task completed *inside TODAY* (never archived on the real board) never changes that — so the periodic `syncDropbox()` merge is the only thing that ever learns about the completion, and nothing else in the tick re-filters `trelloTasks` against the corrected `doneIds` afterward (`mergeRemoteData()` never touches `trelloTasks` at all). This meant the phantom card could resurface through ordinary daily use — another device completing a Trello-linked task, this device's next periodic sync tick merging that completion — not just a one-time fresh install, which is why the original fix didn't hold. Verified `mergeRemoteData()` is fully synchronous end to end (no `await` inside it) before concluding it was safe to call `_reconcileTrelloAfterMerge()` immediately after — `doneIds` and `today_checked_ids` are both guaranteed correct by the time it returns, no race window. Added the call to two sites: `syncDropbox()`'s `fromSync` branch (`index.html:8947`, the actual everyday-use trigger) and the manual "Restore from Dropbox" branch (`index.html:~8965`, which doesn't call `mergeRemoteData()` at all — wholesale-overwrites `doneIds` instead, same bug class, needed its own call). Deliberately did NOT add it to the `online` reconnect handler (`index.html:9345`) — traced that path and found it already self-corrects a few lines later via its own existing unconditional `loadTrello()` call, which reads `doneIds` live after the merge has already finished; adding a reconcile call there would only shave a few hundred milliseconds of stale flicker, not fix a persistence bug, so it was left alone. |
| **2.40.0** | **Feature: poem share. Fix: habit/focus milestones going permanently silent past their top tier.** Poem share (Backlog: Poem share, gate met early 2026-07-21 when the poem's own W3 verdict landed) — a quiet underlined link ("Share this poem") in the About panel, next to the poem itself (not the splash — that's a transitional moment, not one for intent-driven actions). Uses `navigator.share()` where available, falling back to the same clipboard-copy chain the task-copy button already uses (`writeText` → `execCommand` fallback), with the button label swapping to "Copied" briefly on the fallback path. Content is poem text + author + one closing line naming the app and its URL (`today-here.netlify.app`, given by Can — no link invented), no hard CTA, no server round-trip, no share-count tracking. Milestone fix: both `habitMilestones` (`[100, 50, 30, 14, 7]`) and `focusMilestone` (`[100, 50, 25, 10]`) used `.find()` over a fixed descending list — once a run/total passed the highest rung (100), every future check matched the same `100` forever, and the gate (`stored < crossed`) could never re-fire since `100 < 100` is always false. Not a deliberate design choice, an unhandled edge case — surfaced when Can's own dump showed `focusMilestone: 100` and asked "why is it retired forever." Fixed both to switch to open-ended round-tier math once past the top of the original list (`Math.floor(n / 50) * 50` for habits, `Math.floor(n / 100) * 100` for focus) — keeps noticing indefinitely instead of going silent at the ceiling. Existing users sitting exactly at the old ceiling value don't get a false immediate re-fire: the new math resolves to the same tier until the next real threshold is crossed. |
| **2.39.4** | **Feature: Noticed signals now show cross-device the same day they first fire, not just on the device where they fired.** Can's follow-up on v2.39.3: "should show on other devices too, at least on the same day." v2.39.3 made the show-once gate (`appMemory.noticed`) device-local to fix BUG-058's "shown once across all devices combined" bug — correct, but the pendulum swung further than intended: it also meant a signal that fired on one device would *never* show on a second device, not even the same day. Cataloguing all 7 signals found they reduce to just two underlying shapes, not seven distinct ones: a **scalar gate** (single global value compared to last-shown — season, streak proximity, peak hour, week theme, focus milestone) and a **per-entity map** (habit milestones, revived-task-done — needed only because those two can have multiple simultaneous instances, e.g. several habits). Added a second, narrower synced field — `appMemory.noticedDates` — recording *only* "date this occurrence first fired, on any device" per signal-occurrence key (e.g. `peak:14`, `habit:<id>:30`, `theme:<weekKey>`; the key always includes the value being gated on, not just the signal name, so a later occurrence of the same signal type — e.g. peak hour shifting again — gets its own fresh key rather than being blocked by an old one). New `_noticedEligible()`/`_noticedStamp()` helpers in `assets/insights.js`, applied identically to all 7 signal blocks: a signal only shows if nobody's recorded a date for its key yet, or it was recorded *today*; a device checking in on a later day than a signal first fired anywhere stays quiet rather than resurfacing something stale. `mergeRemoteData()` (`index.html`) merges `appMemory.noticedDates` earliest-date-wins per key — deliberately separate from, and narrower than, the `appMemory.noticed` merge that stays removed (v2.39.3): only a date travels, never the actual shown content, so no risk of reintroducing BUG-058's content-divergence problem. Rides in the existing `memory` backup blob — no new payload field. |
| **2.39.3** | **Fix: Noticed showed on one desktop PWA but not another (BUG-058's sync, reverted).** Can's report: got a Noticed line on one device today, nothing on the other. Root cause: `appMemory.noticed` (the show-once bookkeeping for every Noticed signal) has been synced across devices since v2.36.3 (BUG-058) — deliberately, at the time, because two devices could otherwise show different, contradictory observations for the same underlying pattern. But the side effect: "shown once" became "shown once across all devices combined," not once per device — whichever device opens About first silently consumes the notification for every other device too, even though that device's own user never actually saw it. Reverted the merge specifically (`mergeRemoteData`'s `appMemory.noticed` spread, `index.html`) — but not wholesale: the underlying *data* every signal reads (habit completions, focus minutes, peak hour, the week-theme AI text) stays fully synced, so two devices that do show a signal always show identical content. Only the "have I shown you this yet" gate is device-local again. `remote.noticed` still rides along in the backup payload as part of the whole `appMemory` blob — harmless, just unused on the reading side now. Updated four stale references to the old synced behavior (`insights.js` comment, two `Performance-audit.md` lines, `Personalization.md`'s inventory row — the last one had actually said "device-local" this whole time, a comment that was never updated when BUG-058 shipped, coincidentally accurate again now). |
| **2.39.2** | **Fix: cryptic "Unexpected token 'E'... is not valid JSON" error on Dropbox token refresh.** Can's report. Root cause: `_dropboxEnsureToken()` called `res.json()` on the token-refresh fetch unconditionally, with no guard. Our own function (`netlify/functions/dropbox-refresh.js`, checked) always returns valid JSON in every path — but Netlify's own platform can return a plain-text error page instead of invoking the function at all (a timeout, crash, or platform-level failure), and that isn't JSON. `res.json()` throws a `SyntaxError` on that response, which surfaced as the literal parse error rather than a meaningful message. Compounding this: if both retry attempts failed this way, the code fell out of the loop having only logged the cryptic error — the token was never actually marked expired, so `Connections` silently kept showing stale state instead of reflecting that Dropbox needed reconnecting. Fix: read the response as text first, parse only if it looks like JSON; a non-JSON body now throws a readable `HTTP <status> — <snippet>` message and falls through to the existing retry path (the transient-failure case the retry already existed for, e.g. Netlify cold starts per BUG-003) instead of crashing on the parse. A *clean* JSON response indicating failure (e.g. a genuinely revoked refresh token) still marks the token expired immediately with no retry, unchanged from before — retrying an auth rejection was never going to help. If both attempts fail via the non-JSON path, the token now correctly gets marked expired after the loop exhausts, so Connections reflects reality. |
| **2.39.1** | **Fix: morning nudge could mention a task already completed.** Can's report: the nudge picked up a task that was done *before* the nudge actually showed up. Root cause: `day_nudge_ai_<date>` is generated once and cached for the rest of the day, never revalidated — if the AI's sentence references a task and the user finishes it after generation but before actually looking at the banner (generated at 8am, first opened at 9am, task done at 8:05am), the cached text describes already-finished work. Text-matching the AI's sentence against done-task text was ruled out as a fix — the AI only quotes a short fragment of a task's text per its own instructions, not the full string, so an `includes()` check would miss real matches. Fix: stamp `doneIds.size` at generation time (`day_nudge_done_count_<date>`); when reading a cached nudge, compare against the current count — if more tasks are done now than when the text was written, something the AI saw as pending may since be finished, so the cache is discarded and regenerated instead of shown. Local-only for now (not synced across devices) — the reported case was single-device, same-day; cross-device count sync is a possible follow-up if it turns out to matter. |
| **2.39.0** | **Feature: Noticed's "theme of the week" replaced with an AI-crafted observation, not a keyword count.** Can's follow-up on the v2.37.9 stopword fix: even with generic verbs filtered out, "noticing only a word doesn't deliver much value" — a bare repeated word is a stat ("'cleanup' keeps coming up"), never an actual insight, no matter how good the stopword list gets. This is the same Wallpaper Test failure shape as the old week-narrative lines removed in v2.17.66 (Philosophy.md case law): rule-based phrasing stops delivering after the first read. Rather than remove the signal, reused the exact once-per-period AI-generate-cache-sync pattern already proven three times over (`day_nudge_ai`, `week_reflection`, `monday_intention`, BUG-057). New `_fetchWeekThemeAI()` (`index.html`) reads the week's real completed-task texts (`appMemory.recentCompletedTasks`, last 7 days) and asks the model to name a genuine pattern grounded in what was actually done — never a fabricated specific — replying with a sentinel ("none") when nothing genuine stands out, so a quiet week stays quiet rather than forcing an insight. Generation triggers once per week from `renderInfoStats()` (mirroring the Sunday-reflection block immediately above it: check cache → fetch if absent → cache + live-update About if still open), gated by a negative-cache flag (`week_theme_tried_<weekKey>`) so a week with no real pattern doesn't retry the AI call on every subsequent open. New `week_theme_ai` field in the Dropbox backup payload, keyed per calendar week (not per day like its siblings, since it needs to stay stable across all 7 days) — remote-always-wins merge, same reasoning as `week_reflection`/`monday_intention`. `_noticedLines()`'s theme-of-week block (`assets/insights.js`) shrank from a ~20-line deterministic word-frequency counter to a 6-line cache-read + delta-gate, matching every other signal's shape. |
| **2.38.8** | **Fix: morning nudge showed yesterday's cached AI text after the tab stayed open across midnight.** Can's report: a task he'd completed before midnight was described as still pending, and the task count was wrong (16 vs. actual 12). Root cause: `checkNewDay()` (`index.html:9031`) handles the full day-rollover cleanup — tasks, habits, Trello, stats — but never touched the nudge banner. The AI nudge's cache key (`day_nudge_ai_<date>`) is correctly date-scoped by design, but nothing re-checks it against the new date once midnight passes during an already-open session — the banner's DOM just keeps showing whatever was rendered pre-midnight. Compounding this: the `_nudgeRendered` session guard added in v2.38.4 (to stop cross-call-site re-renders) is a per-page-load flag — once the pre-midnight nudge rendered, that flag was already `true`, so even calling `checkDayNudge()` on rollover would have silently no-op'd. Fix: `checkNewDay()` now resets `_nudgeRendered`/`_nudgeRacing` to `false` and calls `checkDayNudge()` as part of its day-boundary sequence, alongside the existing task/habit/Trello/stats refresh — so a tab left open overnight gets a genuinely fresh nudge generation for the new day, not a replay of the old one. |
| **2.38.7** | **Fix: dragged manual task snaps back to old position ~1s later (drag jump-back).** Can's report, noticed after v2.38.5. Root cause: `mergeRemoteData()` ordered manual tasks remote-order-wins with no recency guard. The `_pendingBackup` flag prevents the 7s ticker from reverting a fresh drag, but the **initial load pull** and **reconnect pull** don't check it. On a warm refresh (reopened within 30 min → splash skipped → app interactive instantly) the initial Dropbox pull is still in flight when the user drags; it resolves carrying the pre-drag order and applies it over the drag. Trello order got a recency guard back in BUG-042; manual order never did. v2.38.5's earlier sync kickoff shifted *when* the initial pull lands, which is why it surfaced then — but the missing guard is the real, pre-existing flaw. **Fix (mirrors BUG-042):** new `today_manual_order_at` ISO stamp set on every manual reorder (drop handler + touch `_saveOrder`), synced as `manual_order_at` (schema 5.3 → 5.4). In `mergeRemoteData`, the order basis is chosen by recency — remote wins by default, but a strictly-newer local reorder stamp keeps the local sequence for shared tasks (remote-only additions still append at end, local-only after that, all zone/delete filters unchanged); on remote-wins the device adopts the remote stamp so the next merge compares correctly. Absent stamp (old backup) → `''` → remote wins = exact prior behavior, no migration. Fixes every pull path (initial, ticker, reconnect, wake), not just the one that surfaced. Detail → `architecture/Sync.md`. |
| **2.38.6** | **Fix: morning nudge kept defaulting to the top task despite v2.36.6's anti-echo instruction.** Can's report: it always mentions task 1, wanted it to look at the whole list and surface the most important/hard task instead. Root cause: the prompt (`_fetchDayNudgeAI`) contained a contradiction — it told the model *"position 1 is where the user put their priority"* in the same breath as *"do not echo the top item just because it is first."* The model resolved that the easy way: keep mentioning task 1, just justified as "priority" instead of "position," technically obeying the anti-echo clause's literal wording while still landing on the same task every time. TODAY has no priority/due-date/label field by design (Philosophy.md walls), so "importance" can only be inferred from behavior, never looked up — the fix reframes what's inferred from, not what's stored. Removed the position-as-priority framing entirely (facts block now says "in the order the user arranged things," no "position reflects intent" language). Instruction now explicitly ranks signals: focus sessions spent without being done ("stuck, not just old") as the strongest hard-task signal; revived tasks and overdue Trello cards next; position is named explicitly as sequence, not importance, and forbidden as a standalone reason. Same verbatim-quote requirement and quiet-when-nothing-stands-out fallback kept from v2.36.6. |
| **2.38.5** | **Perf: sync now starts right after `init()` instead of waiting for `window.load`.** Can's question: shouldn't sync begin as soon as the splash animation starts, so it's mostly ready by the time you land in the app? Traced the risk before implementing (Non-Delegation Zone — sync merge logic): `window.load` waits for *every* subresource — both fonts, both icon PNGs, the OG-image meta tag's image, all 11 splash launch-screen PNGs — while the splash animation itself only waits on fonts (`document.fonts.load()` raced against a 2000ms ceiling). That gap meant sync sat idle even after everything the splash actually needed was ready. Splash dismissal already only depends on sync *finishing* (`_appLoadDone`), not on when it started, so starting earlier can only shorten the wait, never lengthen it. Traced two things that could have broken: `startTicker()`'s rev-seed-before-first-tick ordering (safe — it's called from inside the same async function being moved, so the internal sequence travels with it unchanged) and `_appReady` (safe — gated on splash-dismiss completing, not sync start time). Removed the `window.addEventListener('load', ...)` wrapper around the sync-kickoff block; it now runs as a plain block right after `init()`, still wrapped in the same `setTimeout(0)` yield the code already used to avoid blocking first paint. `init()`'s own synchronous execution still guarantees the sync code's `await`ed continuations can't run before `init()` finishes — nothing about that ordering changed. |
| **2.38.4** | **Fix: morning nudge could swap content a second after it appeared.** Can's report on desktop: the nudge shows a message, then the content changes mid-way, a second later. Root cause: `checkDayNudge()` has multiple call sites (init, wake, Dropbox restore, the post-sync load handler), and `_raceAINudge()`'s "settled" flag only prevents a swap *within one call's own race* — it can't stop a later, separate call site from finding a freshly-written cache (written by an earlier call's AI fetch resolving just after its own 1s timeout had already shown the rule-based fallback) and re-rendering with the new text. Concretely: call site A's race times out at 1s and shows the fallback; its AI fetch keeps running in the background and writes the real text to cache moments later; call site B (e.g. the ticker's first tick, ~2s after load, which always treats a null `lastDropboxRev` baseline as "changed") runs `checkDayNudge()` again, finds that fresh cache, and re-displays it — a visible swap the "never replace once shown" design was supposed to prevent, just from a call site it didn't know about. Fixed with two session-level guards (not per-race): `_nudgeRendered` blocks any further render once one has happened this page load, from any call site; `_nudgeRacing` stops a second call site from starting its own parallel race while one is already in flight, avoiding a wasted duplicate AI fetch too. |
| **2.38.3** | **Fix: faint shadow over the top task on hover.** Can's report. Root cause: `.section-header::after`'s fade gradient (z-index 9, deliberate design — comment says "tasks dissolve under the pinned label instead of clipping at a hard edge") always overlaps the top ~10px of whatever sits directly beneath it, regardless of scroll position. At rest, the task's background blends into the gradient invisibly; `.task:hover`'s `--surface` background contrasts against the same permanent overlap enough to read as a visible shadow band — a rendering artifact of an always-on effect, not something tied to scrolling at all. Fixed by giving `.task:hover` `z-index: 10` so the hovered task paints above the gradient layer instead of underneath it. The gradient itself — and its behavior while actually scrolling — is untouched; this only changes stacking order for the hover state. |
| **2.38.2** | **Fix: "Add tasks" in meeting review did nothing.** Can's report. Root cause: a regression from v2.37.4's speaker-tracked attribution work — `_meetingAccept()`'s new attribution-counting block referenced a bare `state.items` copy-pasted from `_meetingRenderReview(state)`'s body, but `_meetingAccept()` has no `state` parameter or local variable. That's a `ReferenceError` the instant the button is clicked, thrown inside a `forEach` callback with nothing to catch it — the whole function aborts before `manualTasks.push()` ever runs, so every accepted item silently vanished. Fixed by referencing `_mtg.items` (the actual live object) instead, guarded with `&& _mtg` since `_mtg` is only set during a live/just-ended meeting. **Also:** the meeting review panel (`#meetingPanel`) now keeps its head ("Meeting" eyebrow, "From your call" title) and its Add/Discard action row fixed in place while only `#meetingItems` scrolls — was previously one scrolling unit (`overflow-y: auto` on the whole panel), same structure the triage overlay still uses today. Converted to `display:flex; flex-direction:column` with `flex-shrink:0` on the head and actions and `overflow-y:auto; min-height:0` on the items list — the `min-height:0` is what lets a flex child actually shrink below its content size and scroll instead of just growing the panel. |
| **2.38.1** | **Polish: two proactive AI observations tuned after a direct review.** Can asked whether `big_clear`/`yesterday_win` and the reactive/intentional day-shape observations needed optimizing, following the v2.38.0 Noticed-expansion work. Findings: (1) `yesterday_win` is already rare by construction (needs the 5+-in-a-day moment AND falls on the one eligible calendar day) but was `priority: 'medium'` — a 50% coin flip on top of an already-sparse signal meant real, rare moments could go unmentioned. Bumped to `high`. (2) the day-shape observation (reactive vs. intentional, `assets/insights.js`) reads a *standing* behavioral state, not a one-time event — once a real habit crosses the threshold it can stay crossed for weeks, and the only throttle was a 24h same-type cooldown + 20% daily roll. Expected recurrence: same wording, roughly every 5 days, for as long as the habit holds — a real Wallpaper Test violation (Philosophy.md: "same trigger, same shape, same takeaway every time"). Fixed with the same mechanism the Noticed peak-hour signal already uses: new `appMemory.patterns.dayShapeState` tracks the last-shown state ('reactive'/'intentional'/null), and the observation only fires again when that state actually *changes* — not on mere persistence. A borderline day near the threshold doesn't cause re-firing since the state only updates on a genuine crossing, not every evaluation. |
| **2.38.0** | **Feature: Noticed gains two new signals — revived-task completion and total focus milestones.** Can's follow-up on v2.37.9's stopword fix: "current Noticed isn't working... I want more smarter, more insightful things to read there." Ran the inventory in `Personalization.md` against what's already collected but unused: two strong candidates surfaced, two others turned out to already exist as *proactive AI observations* elsewhere (`big_clear`/"you cleared N things yesterday" and `reactive_pattern` — both live in `assets/insights.js`'s `_getObservation()`-family functions, a different surface from Noticed) — adding those to Noticed too would restate the same fact in two places, so they were dropped rather than duplicated. Shipped: (1) **revived task finished** — extends v2.35.2's nudge signal ("the choice was theirs, already made") into a one-time Noticed line when a deliberately-revived task gets completed same-day; (2) **total focus milestone** — crosses 10h/25h/50h/100h of lifetime focus, parallel structure to the existing habit-streak milestone, distinct from `_memoryForAI()`'s standing "you've focused for X+ hours" line (which restates every AI call and is never shown verbatim to the user). Both follow the existing delta-gating rule — fire once, never restate. Noticed now has 7 signal types total: season moment, habit milestone, streak proximity, peak hour, theme-of-week, revived-done, focus-milestone.
